them on the same system board
Source: Fibermall.
Active copper cables occupy the middle of the data center interconnect spectrum, sitting
above passive DACs and below optical solutions in reach, power, and cost. Two
architectures matter for investors, with AECs using retimers, equalization, and clock data
recovery to restore signal integrity across longer copper links, while ACCs use a lower-
power redriver approach that amplifies the analog signal but has less ability to remove

jitter. AEC is currently the dominant active copper configuration because it offers stronger
signal integrity, with typical reach of roughly 7 to 9 meters and support for intra-rack and
rack-to-rack AI connectivity, while ACC is more focused on shorter intra-rack links of
roughly 3 meters where very low power and latency are the priorities. Investor relevance
is tied to AI cluster architecture, where copper remains attractive for short-reach scale-up

and selected scale-out links because it can deliver better reliability, lower power, and
lower total cost of ownership than laser-based optical modules when distance
More reports @wechat: Hillwood2024
0N÷P<vî_U0Qettÿ
https://www.valuelist.cn

### Page 15

M
Idea
Morgan Stanley Research 15
requirements are manageable. AECs can reduce interconnect power by roughly 50%
versus pluggable optical transceivers, are materially smaller and lighter than passive
DACs, and are gaining traction as clusters scale from 400G to 800G, with 1.6T
commercialization becoming the next step. At 200G per lane, reach likely compresses
from roughly 7 meters toward closer to 5 meters, but the value proposition can
strengthen because higher speeds increase the need for signal conditioning and drive ASP

uplift for active cable silicon. Adoption also appears increasingly broad across
hyperscalers and NeoClouds, with active copper becoming a standard option for dense in-
rack and multi-rack deployments where customers want to avoid the cost, power, and
operational complexity of optics. From a stock perspective, active copper should be
viewed as complementary to LPO, NPO, and CPO rather than directly competitive, as AI
data centers are likely to use a heterogeneous mix of interconnects based on reach, power

budget, latency, reliability, and serviceability. Optical will remain essential as distances
increase and scale-up domains expand across racks, but active copper should retain an
important role inside the rack and near-rack environment, supporting vendors exposed to
retimers, redrivers, cable assemblies, and validated active cable platforms.
Exhibit 17: ACC prioritizes efficiency inside the rack, while AEC extends reach at higher
power and latency

Source: Marvell
OCS, or optical circuit switching, is  a higher-efficiency optical networking layer for AI
clusters, routing light directly from one port to another rather than relying on EPS, or
electronic packet switching, which converts traffic into electrical signals for processing
inside traditional switch silicon. Investor relevance centers on lower power, lower
forwarding latency, and better long-term upgrade economics, since an OCS can often

support higher link speeds over time without requiring a full switch silicon refresh.
Adoption is most compelling in AI training environments where traffic patterns are
relatively predictable, making OCS a good fit for scale-up pods, upper layers of scale-out
networks, and large inter-data-center transfers where bandwidth, power, and network
More reports @wechat: Hillwood2024
0N÷P<vî_U0Qettÿ
https://www.valuelist.cn

### Page 16

M
Idea
16
TCO matter more than ultra-fast packet-by-packet reconfiguration. Within OCS, micro-
electro-mechanical systems, or MEMS, is the most mature architecture today and uses
tiny movable mirrors to steer light between ports; the advantage is faster switching versus
liquid-crystal alternatives, lower insertion loss, and earlier volume readiness, which makes
MEMS better suited for near-term hyperscale deployments. Liquid crystal on silicon, or

LCoS, takes a different approach by using voltage-controlled liquid crystal to change how
light is directed through the switch; its main advantage is longer expected service life,
which can improve ownership cost over time, but switching latency is slower and
manufacturing still requires precise optical alignment. Waveguide-based OCS and
piezoelectric OCS are more future-oriented approaches, with waveguide designs offering
faster switching potential but currently facing higher insertion loss and cost, while

piezoelectric designs offer very low insertion loss but remain expensive and less mature.
For optical suppliers, the key point is that OCS expands the revenue opportunity beyond
transceivers and CPO light sources into higher-value systems and precision optical
content, including MEMS mirrors, LCoS switching elements, collimator arrays, calibration
systems, beam displacers, and other optical assemblies. LITE appears more levered to
MEMS-based OCS, while COHR is more relevant to LCoS-based OCS and broader

precision optical content. Demand is still concentrated and execution-heavy, but OCS
should be viewed as a complementary growth vector alongside pluggable optics, NPO,
and CPO, giving optical suppliers another path to monetize AI networking growth as
customers optimize for power, latency, upgrade flexibility, and total cost of ownership.
Exhibit 18: Google’s Palomar OCS highlights its leadership in bringing optical circuit
switching from lab architecture to production-scale data center networks

Source: Google
Common Questions / Debates
Is the biggest hurdle to CPO the technology or just not wanting to be locked into a
proprietary ecosystem? There are a few considerations here: 1) Vendor lock-in, 2) Cost, 3)
Feasibility. We would note  the cost / feasibility points have traditionally ranked above
vendor lock-in, but as we near CPO solutions, that has become more significant. The
copper and optical transceiver ecosystems (for scale-out in the case of optical

transceivers) are diversified, and while supply chains have remained tight, produce far
More reports @wechat: Hillwood2024
0N÷P<vî_U0Qettÿ
https://www.valuelist.cn

### Page 17

M
Idea
Morgan Stanley Research 17
lower margins than XPU vendors today. As a result, concentrating more of the economics
into the XPU vendor has generally been viewed unattractively. Additionally, with
packaging ecosystem needing to come together, the second biggest consideration has
been feasibility and whether the risk / reward of adoption works (e.g. Google has often
commented that power savings are not worth the additional quality / failure risk).

Can CPO be avoided longer term? While we left OFC this year noting that there was
meaningful resistance from the hyperscalers to CPO, NVDA's promotion of CPO with the
Spectrum-6 switch as part of their Vera Rubin Ultra design introductions kept investors
encouraged on the prospects. We would note, that do the extent that cluster sizes are not
important, you can push out CPO adoption for a very long time. Additionally, if just
passing elephant flows around, OCS can be a somewhat more reliable option (even if

limited). However, if you 1) want to be able to scale XPUs in a cluster, 2) run into I/O
problems, you will eventually need to move to optical vs. copper interconnects as you
move towards cross rack clusters.
Is the scale-up Ethernet opportunity actually attractive for branded switching
vendors? In general, given less complicated requirements within a scale-up network, as
just transferring traffic within a GPU / XPU cluster, the switching needs are less

complicated. The speed of the chips needs to be high, but the software can be more
simplified. This lends itself to more simplified switching, which can largely be done by
white / blue boxes. As a result, while there is a lot of volume in the scale-up Ethernet
opportunity, we don't find it as attractive for branded vendors like ANET until we get to
multi-rack solutions in the 2028+ period.
Do we need as big of scale-up clusters with inference or just with training? In general,

the importance of the size of the scale-up cluster is more important with training vs.
inference. Reason being, in training, every accelerator is working towards solving the same
problem, with advanced synchronization and latency needs that can only be achieved in a
scale-up cluster.  However, frontier models still want 100+ XPUs for inference in the scale-
up network, which as a result, will continue to advance the conversation on scale-up
architectures, regardless of whether mix of workloads adjusts between training /

inference.

Exhibit 20: It Will Take Time, but Copper Eventually Reaches Limits within Scale-Up

Networks
Source: 650 Group.
What about Amazon's Resilient Network Graph (RNG)?  To give a basic description,
Amazon noted in an academic paper over the last couple of weeks that they were
deploying a new network architecture in most of their data centers that moves away from
traditional architectures (e.g. fat tree or tiers of switching / routing) and moves towards a
flatter, more mesh network, where adaptive routing and ShuffleBoxes help with traffic

management. This network design requires significant adaptive routing / software
expertise, a move away from recent history, where more networking has been added, but
with more open source software. Given value of software work Amazon has done, and
the proprietary nature of their network designs, we don't yet see major adoption
beyond Amazon.
AWS’s RNG (Resilient Network Graphs) architecture marks a material shift from
conventional fat-tree data center networking, replacing a hierarchical fabric where traffic

moves through upper aggregation layers with a flatter quasi-random graph designed to
create broader path diversity across routers. Conventional fat-tree networks are easier to
route but require additional router layers, can concentrate congestion near the top of the
hierarchy, and create more fragility when important routers fail. RNG uses quasi-random
connectivity, ShuffleBox passive optical components, and Spraypoint routing to make
flatter networks practical at production scale, reducing router count by 69%, improving

throughput by up to 33%, and projecting a 40% reduction in network equipment
electricity consumption. Adoption has moved quickly from production validation to global
default design, with AWS’s first quasi-random production network going live near Dublin
at the end of 2024, refined through two additional deployments, and becoming the
default architecture for most new AWS data centers globally by April 2026. Moving from
initial deployment to default architecture in about 18 months suggests a high level of

confidence in the design and highlights how aggressively AWS is reworking the physical
network layer to support AI-scale infrastructure.

Stock Takeaways / CPO Adoption EPS Analysis
2026–2027: Copper is the story. The emergence of alternative XPUs creates a greenfield
opportunity for networking suppliers outside of NVIDIA's proprietary NVLink ecosystem.
While NVIDIA will continue to capture the majority of scale-up spending through NVLink,

we see significant opportunity across AMD, AWS Trainium and the growing custom ASIC
ecosystem, where networking standards remain in flux and multiple suppliers can
participate (see What are the Solutions).
ALAB: Pure-play AI networking exposure; attention shifts from Scorpio to ecosystem
breadth. We expect Scorpio to drive a strong 2H26 ramp, but investor attention is already
moving toward UALink adoption and customer diversification beyond the initial

hyperscaler programs. We continue to view Astera as one of the best pure-play exposures
to AI connectivity and remain impressed by its ability to expand content per accelerator
from roughly $50–$100 at IPO to more than $1,000. Scorpio X is now shipping, providing
the next major leg of content growth. The copper-to-optics transition is an important
watch item, but we do not view replacement as imminent. Copper should retain
meaningful intra-rack runway, while Astera builds its optical roadmap through their small

acquisitions and organic development. Near-term execution remains centered on Scorpio
and broadening the customer base; optics provides longer-term optionality rather than an
immediate risk to the core thesis.
SMTC: A developing beneficiary of both copper longevity and linear optics. Semtech
offers exposure to both sides of the connectivity transition. CopperEdge extends short-
reach copper at up to 90% lower power than DSP-based AECs, supporting our view that

copper remains viable longer than many expect. At the same time, FiberEdge and
DirectEdge TIAs and drivers benefit from the move toward 800G, 1.6T and linear optical
architectures. Semtech’s components complement high-quality SerDes rather than
competing with switch or DSP vendors, allowing the company to benefit as lane rates
increase regardless of the underlying platform. The HieFo acquisition adds InP lasers and
gain chips, expanding Semtech from analog front-end components toward a more

complete optical chipset for 3.2T, NPO and CPO applications. Near-term upside depends
More reports @wechat: Hillwood2024
0N÷P<vî_U0Qettÿ
https://www.valuelist.cn

### Page 20

M
Idea
20
on converting CopperEdge and FiberEdge design wins into revenue; longer term, HieFo
could materially increase content per optical module and improve Semtech’s strategic
position in the supply chain.
AVGO:  Scale-up networking is an underappreciated extension of the XPU opportunity.
The Broadcom narrative remains centered on custom XPUs, but we see a sizable
opportunity to pull through scale-up networking content alongside those wins. Because

Broadcom designs the ASIC and owns a broad portfolio spanning Ethernet switches, PCIe,
SerDes, NICs and optics, it is well positioned to bundle the surrounding connectivity
architecture for its customers. This gives Broadcom an advantage as newer custom ASIC
programs move from silicon development into rack-scale deployments. Broadcom is also a
technology leader in CPO and next-generation switching, despite taking a conservative
view on adoption. Management believes 200G, and eventually 400G, SerDes can extend

copper within the rack, supporting our view that broad scale-up CPO adoption is more
likely around 2028–29. Scale-out customers can adopt CPO earlier for power savings, but
the economics may not yet justify a broad transition.
MRVL: Broad fabric optionality, with optics as the core differentiator. Marvell already
has a leading position in scale-out optics and is positioning across all three potential scale-
up paths: UALink, ESUN and NVLink Fusion. Its recently announced strategic partnership

with NVIDIA reinforces Marvell’s role in custom XPUs, scale-up networking and silicon
photonics, while the Celestial AI acquisition adds a purpose-built photonic fabric for
optical scale-up. Marvell’s ability to support multiple fabrics limits dependence on any
single standard, while Celestial AI provides a credible path toward leadership in merchant
optical scale-up as the market transitions around 2028. The key debate is execution -
integrating the acquired technology and converting its broad customer pipeline into

volume deployments.
CRDO (NC) AEC leadership today, with optics emerging as a second growth engine.
Credo remains one of the cleanest exposures to copper durability through its ZeroFlap
AEC franchise. The current revenue base remains primarily scale-out, but Blue Heron gives
Credo direct scale-up optionality across UALink, ESUN and Ethernet as those architectures
begin to ramp. Credo’s optical expansion also reduces the risk from an eventual copper-to-

optics transition. The DustPhotonics acquisition, ZeroFlap optical transceivers and new
optical DSP portfolio broaden the company’s exposure across 800G, 1.6T and 3.2T
connectivity. Management expects more than 80% FY2027 revenue growth and over
$600 million from optical products, making optics a meaningful second growth engine
rather than simply a defensive hedge. The key watch item is execution across several new
product categories while sustaining momentum in the core AEC business.

APH (NC)
2028+: Optics becomes the opportunity. While timing of optics coming further into
scale-up is up for debate, one where we believe the adoption will begin in '28, but be slow
given production capacity with Ultra Rubin, the fact that optics will eventually come into
scale-up is more fact. If you want to have larger GPU / XPU cluster sizes and you want to
resolve eventual I/O bottlenecks, network builders will have to incorporate optics. As we

move towards Feynman and Interposer architectures in 2029 and beyond periods, optics
in scale-up becomes unavoidable.
Rebuilt LITE / COHR / GLW models sensitize for CPO adoption. While we previously had a

Morgan Stanley Research 21
CPO line-item for LITE / COHR models, we have more accurately built these models to
reflect CPO penetration percentages.
GLW: Cleanest architecture-agnostic way to play optical scale-up, with management
explicitly framing 2028 as the likely starting point and expecting early adoption to look
hybrid rather than a binary copper-to-optics transition. Passive photonics content across
FAUs, PMF, ELS fiber and GlassBridge should remain relevant across 2D CPO, 2.5D CPO

and NPO, while the company’s support for open-reference architectures helps preserve
ecosystem breadth even as optics moves closer to the package.
LITE: Levered to the optical engine and laser content required as scale-up bandwidth
moves beyond what pluggables can efficiently support, with management calling NPO an
intermediate step and CPO the likely next-generation convergence point. Scale-up optical
shipments are expected to begin in late 2027, with products in market in early 2028, and

management sizes the first scale-up opportunity at 3–4x initial scale-out CPO, with a
much larger lane opportunity as optics moves inside the rack.
COHR: Broad exposure across the full CPO/NPO component stack, including high-power
CW lasers, external laser source modules, FAUs, polarization-maintaining fiber, isolators
and thermoelectric coolers. Management has been the most aggressive on scale-up sizing,
describing the opportunity as orders of magnitude larger than scale-out, while its support

for multiple architectures across InP, silicon photonics and VCSEL-based solutions
positions the company to benefit regardless of whether customers move through NPO, 2D
CPO or more advanced 2.5D CPO structures.
KEYS: Upgrading KEYS to OW as a play on AI investment and broadening architectures vs.
a play on a particular architecture. KEYS sees over half of their revenue come from R&D /
lab use cases, and has seen their  AI revenue (mid-teens % of revenue) increase as not only

have investment cycles shortened, but the variety of architectures has increased (e.g.
Ethernet vs. Infiniband). Bottom line, customers are ramping 800G, accelerating 1.6T R&D,
and already planning for 3.2T, while also optimizing for speed, power, density, scale-up
networking, and scale-out architectures. We believe this concurrency matters because it
raises test intensity, broadens the number of customer programs KEYS can serve, and
reduces reliance on any single product cycle. Faster innovation cadence and greater

system complexity should support a longer growth runway than investors typically assign
to test and measurement. Given there will be a growing mix of all of these architectures,
we prefer the play on the diversity of architectures vs. one particular (since architecture
debate unlikely to be solved for multiple years). We see the path to achieving our PT
through estimate revisions as testing density increases and incremental margins improve.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.

::研究員聯絡方式:: 
研究員 林政謙 
電子信箱 6420@entrust.com.tw

泓德能源(6873)  
-  海外市場獲利貢獻快速成長  
投資建議 
標題  
  泓德能源投資評等建議為買進，目標價102元；隱含漲幅19.2%，預
估2026年稅後EPS為5.37元，投資建議在本益比15-19倍區間操作，主
要理由如下： 
(1) 公司預計2026年台灣太陽能電廠開工238MW、太陽能收購250-
500MW、儲能案廠開工200MW、日本營運/開工容量：83MW／
191MW、澳洲營運／開工容量：121MW／256MW，將在未來
幾年陸續開始認列營收與獲利。 
(2) 子公司星星電力預計在第四季登錄興櫃，受惠國內綠電轉供市場快
速成長，加上日本、澳洲等地的電力交易獲利貢獻，預計到2030
年都可維持快速成長。

內容摘要 
   泓德能源預期工程進度集中在下半年，第四季營收會高於第三季   
  公司2026年主要工程為台灣太陽能電廠開工238MW、儲能案廠開工
200MW，將在未來幾年陸續開始認列營收與獲利，預期工程進度集中
在下半年，第四季營收通常會高於第三季。   
   泓德能源預計海外市場從2026年起將快速成長   
  公司預估2026-2029年全球約 9.4GW，儲能：6.41GW、太陽能：
3.02GW，預估每年約1,528MW進入投產階段，約1,423MW轉為營運
容量，可帶來開發、EPC、設備銷售、管理、維運及電力交易等多重
收入。   
   泓德能源綠電銷售量可望持續成長，總合約度數 161億度   
  公司在發電業務上攜手多間壽險業者組成綠能平台，投資與拓展太陽
光電、漁電共生案場。星星電力在2026年綠電轉供目標為 5-6億度。

:: 訪談內容::

泓德能源逐步轉向表後儲能、電價套利與電力對沖……等市場 
 1. 公司成立於2016年，主要從事能源技術服務，範圍涵蓋電力開發
及工程、 EPC、維運、綠電轉供、 儲能及充電樁，為智慧電力服務
之供應商。 
2. 公司於2022年收購位於台南柳營區全台單一最大 200MW容量儲能
案場，參與輔助服務市場，並與台灣人壽、全球人壽、富邦人壽，
成立星鱻電力股份有限公司，共同耕耘漁電共生。 
3. 公司 目前主要業務包括：太陽光電及儲能案場開發、 EPC工程建
置、案場出售及設備銷售、綠電轉供、資產管理與維運、日本、澳
洲電力交易。 
4. 公司正由過去以表前光電、儲能資產及 EPC工程為主，逐步轉向表
後儲能、AI智慧電力調度、電價套利與電力對沖、資產管理平台、
資料中心與綠氫及綠鋼等大型用電戶直供。 
5. 2025年營收及獲利下降的主因：台灣光電案場受到許可及政策因
素影響而遞延、公司提前擴充日本、澳洲團隊，營業費用上升、澳
洲Templers及日本 Helios等海外資產納入合併，增加利息與業外
支出、海外布局成本先行發生 ，但工程、設備銷售與電力交易收入
尚未充分反映。

泓德能源預期工程進度集中在下半年，第四季營收通常會高於第三季 
 1. 公司2026年主要工程目標：台灣太陽能電廠開工 238MW、太陽能收
購250-500MW 、 儲 能 案 廠 開 工200MW 、 日 本 營 運/ 開 工 容 量 ：
83MW／191MW、澳洲營運／開工容量： 121MW／256MW，將
在未來幾年陸續開始認列營收與獲利。 
2. 公司預期工程進度集中在下半年，第四季營收通常會高於第三季。
2026 年 營 收 結 構 預 估 約 為 ： 台 灣 ：70% ～80% 、 海 外 ：20% ～
30%，2027年後，隨日本及澳洲設備出貨、資產平台及電力交易放
量，海外營收比重將逐步提高。 
3. 星星電力在2026年綠電轉供目標為 5億-6億度，電力交易容量目標：
台灣約260MW、日本接近100MW、澳洲初期約10MW，預計2026
年第四季登錄興櫃。

### Page 3

泓德能源預計海外市場從2026年起將快速成長 
 1. 公司預計2026年各地區主要收入來源:台灣地區有238MW太陽能工
程、200MW儲能工程、RTB案場收購後的EPC收入、星星電力綠電
轉供、五大資產平台開發、工程及管理收入。 
2. 日本：2MW小型儲能案場出售及施工、Helios 50MW案場電力交
易、案場開發服務收入、部分設備銷售與資產管理收入。 
3. 澳洲: Templers 111MW案場的穩定營運收入、開發服務收入、
2026年仍以開發、許可及平台籌組為主，大型設備銷售主要自2027
年後開始。 
4. 公司2026年後成長動能，2026-2029年全球約 9.4GW，儲能：
6.41GW、太陽能：3.02GW，預估每年約1,528MW進入投產階
段，約1,423MW轉為營運容量，2026～2029年建置容量年複合成
長率約 45.5%，可帶來開發、EPC、設備銷售、管理、維運及電力
交易等多重收入。 
5. 台灣太陽能約 2.53GW，儲能約 761MW，2026年預計收購250～
500MW太陽能案場，可快速取得案場開發權、EPC工程訂單、後續
綠電售電權，嘉義大型案場進入建置期，彰化案場規模擴大至
90MW，五大資產平台管理資產金額預計超過 760億元，可持續提
供案場出售、工程及管理收入。 
6. 日本地區2027年後大型儲能工程加速，儲能約 3.26GW、太陽能約 
180MW，年均投產速度約 614MW。 
7. 澳洲地區2027～2029年大型設備出貨高峰，儲能約 2.38GW、太陽
能約 310MW，預計2029年超過 2.5GW完成投產。 
8. 星星電力由低毛利轉供走向高毛利電力交易，台灣綠電轉供規模持
續成長，但毛利率相對較低，未來主要成長來自日本及澳洲電價套
利、AI自動投標、儲能聚合與虛擬電廠、企業備用供電、表後儲能
共享方案。

泓德能源綠電銷售量可望持續成長，總合約度數 161億度 
 1. 公司在發電業務上攜手多間壽險業者組成綠能平台，投資與拓展太
陽光電、漁電共生案場。 
2. 星泓電力 (地面電廠 )：裝置容量 96MW，資產規模 50億，股東結
構：台灣人壽30%、全球人壽30%、康舒20%、泓德20%。 
3. 星鱻電力(漁電共生)： 裝置容量74MW(一期)、189MW(二期)，資
產規模40億(一期)、113億(二期)，股東結構：台灣人壽 30%、全球
人壽30%、富邦人壽30%、泓德10%。 
4. 星德電力(儲能系統)：：E-dReg表前儲能預計101.5MW，資產規模
50億，股東結構：台灣人壽 10%、全球人壽10%、富邦人壽 10%、
泓德67%、士電3%。 
5. 富 邦 能 源( 太 陽 光 電 和 表 前 儲 能) ： 預 計 太 陽 能 和 儲 能 系 統 各
350MW，資產規模預計 400億，股東結構：富邦人壽 90%、泓德
10%。

::近一年個股與大盤報酬率比較::

::研究員聯絡方式:: 
研究員 陳逸民 
電子信箱 9899@entrust.com.tw

大立光(3008)  
-  預期6月至8月拉貨動能月月增高，
第三季產能利用率將滿載  
投資建議

大立光(3008)投資評等強力買進，目標價 5000.0元；隱含報酬率 
26.6%，預估2026年稅後EPS 192.31元，投資建議在本益比21~26倍
區間操作，主要理由如下： 
(1). 預期6月至8月拉貨動能月月增高，第三季產能利用率將滿載。 
(2). 預期大客戶新款手機相機規格持續升級。 
(3). FA預計7月送樣認證，並於今年第三季底架設試量產線。

內容摘要 
  預期6月至8月拉貨動能月月增高，第三季產能利用率將滿載  
 大立光表示，預期7月拉貨動能會比6月好一點，8月會比7月好，第三
季產能利用率將滿載。第三季營收表現尚且看不出來，單季毛利率則
要看良率表現。可變光圈鏡頭預計下半年開始大量出貨，因製程複雜
度提升，產品平均單價（ASP）有望提升。  
  預期大客戶新款手機相機規格持續升級  
大立光表示相較於潛望式鏡頭，可變光圈產品規格比較難，主要是因
為Particle抓很緊，良率剛開始都沒有那麼好，而良率對毛利率影響比
較關鍵，如果良率好，毛利率就會好一點。預期2027年智慧型手機，
潛望式鏡頭會升級，看遠跟看近都會較清晰，主鏡頭也有新的規格。  
  FA預計7月送樣認證，並於今年第三季底架設試量產線  
針對康寧發布晶圓級玻璃光互連平台「Glass Bridge」，大立光表
示，此技術主要是針對Edge Coupling(側邊耦光)架構，市場還是
Surface Grating Coupling(表面光柵耦合；GC)明顯多於EC，就算EC
成功也是兩者並存，大立光會繼續走自己的步調，光纖陣列(FA)預計7
月送樣認證，並於今年第三季底架設試量產線。

### Page 2

:: 訪談內容::

大立光(3008)成立於1987年四
月十七日，主要生產光學鏡頭及
鏡片，應用於掃瞄器、相機、多
功能事務機、液晶投影機、數位
相機、手機鏡頭、背投電視、
DVD讀取頭、光學滑鼠等。公司
為國內生產光學鏡頭及鏡片之領
導廠商。 
 公司簡介: 
 1. 大立光(3008)成立於1987年4月17日，主要生產光學鏡頭及鏡片，
應用於掃瞄器、相機、多功能事務機、液晶投影機、數位相機、手
機鏡頭、背投電視、 DVD讀取頭、光學滑鼠等。公司為國內生產光
學鏡頭及鏡片之領導廠商。 
2. 1991年公司量產出非球面塑膠鏡片，已成為全球主要的塑膠非球面
鏡片生產商。 2002年手機開始加入照相功能，塑膠鏡片具 成本優
勢，超越玻璃鏡片，成為光學鏡頭市場明星商品。 
3. 過去公司所生產之鏡頭多屬應用於數位相機，但在數位相機逐漸普
及、價格滑落、毛利及獲利受到侵蝕下，公司改以搭載於手機的鏡
頭為主要產品，在開發高階產品速度及導入量產之生產能力優於同
業，為其競爭利基。

產品組合: 
 1. 沿用舊鏡頭規格的新機種，一般ASP都會隨時間來降價。同時影響生
產良率的鍍膜、組裝、管理等很多環節都要改善，理論上隨著經驗
值多了會好一些，有助於毛利率，至於外購光學元件主要是幫客戶
做組裝跟著鏡頭一起出貨，大立光只加組裝費，如 果折損率低，大
立光會賺多一點，折損率高就沒得賺。 
2. 2026年第二季 產品出貨量比重： 20MP以上佔10~20%、10M以上
佔50~60%、8M以上佔0~10%、其他佔20~30%。 
3. 2025 年產品出貨量比重： 20MP 以上佔 10~20% 、 10M 以上佔
50~60%、8M以上佔0~10%、其他佔20~30%。 
4. 大立光有一組人專門在車用鏡頭領域，一直都有在交貨，看客戶需
求開案。 
5. 不論中高低階產品，只要價格合理的鏡片，大立光才會有興趣去
做，不會有一定要填滿產能的想法。 
6. 搭載一片模造玻璃（MG）可以讓拍攝效果更好，拉高黑 白對比解析
力的設計值。 MG本身是外購與也有自製， MG自製會在台灣新的四
廠做，lead time 需要6個月。 
產品組合 
年別 2025 % 2026(F) % %

### Page 3

營運狀況: 
 1. 大立光(3008) 2026年6月營收 37.12億元，月增率 -19.18%，年增
率 -10.46%，累計2026年前6月營收為 292.08億元，年增率 
11.26%。2026年6月份營收較5月下滑，主要受排程影響，良率影
響不大。 
2. 2026年第二季營收 136.64億元，季增率 -12%，年增率 17%。毛利 
67.52億元，毛利率 49.4%。營益 51.48億元。業外 9.51億元。稅前
淨利 60.99億元。稅後淨利 46.69億元。EPS 35.72元。 
3. 2026年第二季業外約 9.5億元，其中：利息收入約 9.8億元、匯兌損
失約 1.6億元。 
4. 2026年第二季資本支出為 27.8億元，折舊為 20億元。 
5. 2026年上半年營收 292.08億元，年增率 11%。毛利 144.31億元。
營益 109.60億元。業外 24.26億元。稅前淨利 133.87億元。稅後淨
利 107.93億元。EPS 82.35元。

未來展望 : 
 1. 展望後市，大立光預期2026年7月業績將優於6月，8月將優於7月。 
2. 2026年下半年雖為傳統旺季，但目前看不出 2026年第三季營收能否
維持2026年上半年強勁的年增長趨勢。 
3. 2026年下半年新手機 forecast 數量目前與過去差不多。 
4. 客戶排程有往後延的趨勢，可能使 2026年第四季表現較好；但若終
端銷售不佳，客戶也可能快速下修訂單。 
5. 大立光說明5月、6月、7月營收變化主因是排程關係，良率會有一些
影響，但不是主要因素。第三季毛利率表現還是要看良率，可變光
圈製造難度較高，良率若佳，毛利率會較好。 
6. 原物料價格對公司高階產品影響不大，雖然有部分市場區域幾乎不
開發高階產品，但主要客戶仍持續推進高階產品發展。 
7. 從客戶現階段規劃來看，明年潛望式鏡頭、主鏡頭都可能有 升級趨
勢，如潛望式鏡頭可能會變得更多片（鏡片數增加），以提升手機
看近、看遠時的畫面清晰度。 
8. 手機可變光圈鏡頭：已開發多年，出貨量視客戶拉貨而定。規格大
致確立，檢測要求高。毛利是否優於潛望式鏡頭（ Periscope）仍不
確定，須視量產良率。 
9. 大立光預計2026年第三季底完成 CPO試產線，最快明年中量產，貢
獻營收。除了一家期望量產客戶外，還有其他客戶針對下世代產品
做概念驗證（POC）中。 
10. 關於康寧新發表的Glass Bridge技術影響，大立光表示該技術主要用
於側邊耦光（ Edge Coupling, EC ），而 大立光聚焦表面光柵耦光
（Surface Grating Coupling, GC ），未來一定是兩個技術並存，就
算EC成功也還是會有GC市場。

### Page 4

新技術趨勢發展 :  
 1. 手機可變光圈鏡頭：預計從 2026年第三季開始出貨，價格較好但
良率待觀察。生產難度在於檢測工序，每個光圈檔位都需單獨檢
測，複雜度取決於客戶要求。 
2. 手機潛望式鏡頭：今年規格未升級，未來將朝更高畫素發展以實現
更強變焦。 
3. 手機薄型鏡頭：因市場反應不佳，需求下降。 
4. 手機折疊鏡頭：預計 2026年底出貨，其規格與設計難度與 一般手
機鏡頭相似。 
5. 手機混合鏡頭 (Hybrid Lens) ：需求主要來自高階旗艦機種，尚未
普及至中低階市場。 
6. CPO ( 共封裝光學)：公司自主研發中，難度高於市場現有技術，成
功定義為能證明可大規模量產。 
7. 康寧新發表的Glass Bridge 技術影響，大立光表示目前客戶絕大多
數都還是以GC為主，未來市場GC仍會有一定市占率，甚至可能還
比EC高，且EC方案也還是需要搭配FA產品。 
8. PMLA 與半導體石英玻璃製程之競爭力尚未確認。未來若採用半導
體製程的 PMLA 產品在成本或效能上更具優勢，公司可能轉為直接
採購。 
9. Metalens ( 超透鏡)：非公司專業領域，主要應用於臉部辨識，預
計2026年對業務無貢獻。

手機鏡頭技術趨勢: 
 1. 手機鏡頭的 CIS(CMOS Image Sensor) 影像感測器走向 1”，光學
鏡頭理論上也要Upgrade，但是也要看手機廠客戶的態度。 
2. 潛望式鏡頭仍為高階手機主流規格，不過，目前搭載的稜鏡仍以玻
璃為主，根據供應鏈消息，外傳蘋果、三星及華為都嘗試導入塑膠
稜鏡的可行性，大立光表示目前塑膠稜鏡功能已通過客戶驗證，舊
機種不會用，新機種導入與否仍視客戶需求。 
3. 潛望式長焦鏡頭感測器（sensor）放大，稜鏡要求的功能也提升，
都增加組裝難度，放大倍率Zoom Ratio 是一樣(5X)5倍。主鏡頭最
高檔維持八片塑膠鏡片（8P）設計，超廣角鏡頭沒什麼改變。 
4. 折疊手機與薄型手機都有在談 。折疊手機 有些已有交貨，但量不
大。手機太薄，生產門檻就會增加，若要維持MPF（黑白對比解析
力）又要薄就會很難做，折疊機與客戶討論開發中。 
5. 目前多採 G+P（玻璃＋塑膠）的混合式鏡頭設計，由於 8P鏡頭太
貴，客戶反而不用，高階鏡頭以 1MG＋6P取代7P，模造玻璃自製
或外購都有模造玻璃價格不錯，但增加產能仍要看需求。 
6. 2026年有可變光圈鏡頭蠻大的專案，在旗艦機種的主鏡頭上。

::研究員聯絡方式:: 
研究員 楊富寶 
電子信箱 11116@entrust.com.tw

力積電(6770)  
- 技術躍升與產品點火引領獲利質變 
投資建議 
標題  
 力積電投資評等為買進操作，目標價 85 元，預估 2026 年 EPS 為 5.68 元，
建議在本益比 12-15 倍操作，主要理由如下： 
(1) 技術躍升與大客戶帶動營收質變：力積電透過高密度矽電容技術，成功打
入 Intel EMIB 先進封裝生態系，催化本業由虧轉盈。大客戶強勁拉貨驅
動 12 吋產能全線滿載，帶動獲利本質性大成長，預估 2026 年 EPS 將成
長至 5.68 元，技術與營收結構迎來結構性改變。 
(2) 三大新產品線點火引領獲利躍升：公司全面聚焦邏輯優化、記憶體結構漲
價與 3D AI 先進封裝等三大產品線，高附加價值產品在 4 月及 6 月陸續
放量。這將全面剔除低毛利傳統產能，加上公司啟動結構性漲價 15-
30%，提升獲利品質。

內容摘要 
   1Q26 轉型代工新局 營運動能全面成長   
 力積電 1Q26 獲利強勁， EPS 達 3.36 元，本業因邏輯代工調漲 15-30% 及
記憶體漲價而由虧轉盈，6 月營收年增 68.75%。受惠於 Intel 與 AI PMIC 帶
動，12 吋產能全線滿載，加上處分 P5 廠予美光認列一次性利益，獲利品質
與營運動能明顯改善。   
  三大產品線點火驅動長線高獲利  
 2026 年為力積電轉型「3D AI Foundry」元年，公司全面聚焦三大產品線。
透過 P5 廠轉讓美光實現「資產輕量化」，除成功移除高額折舊負擔、大幅優
化現金流外，更將承接美光 HBM PWF 代工，從競爭轉為共生，擺脫傳統週
期性晶圓廠的標籤。                
   特殊製程放量獲利結構提升  
力積電積極切入美系 AI 供應鏈，其 EMIB 封裝開發的 IPD 矽電容具備大於
1000nF/mm2 全球領先密度，已於 2026 年 4 月放量。搭配獨家 WoW 晶圓
堆疊技術，支援 4-8 層深度異質整合並將能耗降至 < 1 pJ/bit，與 CPO 矽光
子布局共同成為攻克 AI 記憶體牆的核心利器。

### Page 2

:: 訪談內容::

力積電價值轉型，切入 AI 供應
鏈並優化財務結構 
 公司簡介 
 1. 力積電為台灣知名晶圓代工廠，目前營運 2 座 8 吋廠與 4 座 12 吋廠 。公司
於 2025 年啟動「價值轉型」，致力於從成熟製程的價格競爭中脫離，轉向
高附加價值的技術服務 。透過 Open Foundry 模式整合客戶流程，提供客
製化技術平台與矽中介層技術，深化與客戶的共同開發模式 。 
2. 公司優勢在於 3D AI Foundry 技術與 3D WoW（Wafer-on-Wafer）晶圓
堆疊技術 。以 2.5D 中介層量產能力與 HBM 代工，切入 AI 供應鏈，達成
「雙重溢價」模式 。此外，透過與美光的合作綁定，不僅優化財務體質，更
確立了在 AI 記憶體市場的技術領先地位 。 
3. 生產基地布局以台灣為主，廠區分布於新竹、竹南與銅鑼 。新竹與竹南廠專
注於邏輯、功率元件及利基型記憶體代工 ；銅鑼 P5 廠則策略性轉讓予美
光，藉此移除高額折舊負擔並獲取 18 億美元資金，轉向「輕資產、高價
值」營運模式，專注於高毛利產品回流 。

三大核心業務結構質變引領營收
利潤結構性大噴發

產品組合 
 1. 力積電產品組合多元：2025 年各應用營收占比： 邏輯代工 52.8%、記憶
體代工 44.0%、3D AI 技術平台 3.2%。  
2. 邏輯代工業務：2025 年在電源管理與車規晶片健康拉貨下貢獻營收占比
52.8%；2026 年在 AI 伺服器高功率 PMIC 剛性需求與漲價下，預估將成
長 53% 至 營收占比至 55%。受惠新客戶導入，其高成本效益之 12 吋鋁
製程平台將迎來全線滿載。  
3. 記憶體代工業務：2025 年靠著在 ≤1Gb 利基型 DRAM 獲全球 71% 的市
占，為公司注入營收占比 44.0% 的穩固收入；2026 年預估將成長 33.5% 
至 營收占比至 40%。現狀高階車規與工控 NOR Flash 新訂單陸續於大新
竹廠區放量，與利基型記憶體共同成為撐起產能滿載的 關鍵。  
4. 3D AI 技術平台業務：2025 年處於小量階段，營收為占比 3.2%，利用 
WoW 晶圓堆疊與高密度矽電容技術；2026 年迎來爆發元年，營收成長 
120% 至 營收占比 4.79%。主要原因是與愛普合作之 IPD 矽電容正式打入 
Intel 的 EMIB 先進封裝生態系，因應 2H26 AI 伺服器出貨而迎來大客戶擴
大備貨放量潮。 
5. 預估 2026 年各應用營收占比： 邏輯代工 55%、記憶體代工 40%、3D AI 
技術平台 5%。

美洲高效能運算轉單強勁歐美雙
軌去風險策略發酵

地區營收占比 
 1. 2025 年地區營收占比： 美洲占 50.0%、歐洲占 20.0% 及 亞洲與其他占 
30.0%。 
2. 預估 2026 年地區營收占比： 美洲占 55.10%、歐洲占 17.7% 及 亞洲與其
他占 27.2%。

力積電1Q26獲利強勁轉型代工
新局營運動能全面成長 
 營運狀況 
 1.  2026 年 6 月營收為 64.74 億元，MoM +12.19%、YoY +68.75%，累計營
收 YoY +37.81%；1Q26 EPS 3.36 元，QoQ +2,276% 、YoY +1,398% ；
1H26 營運動能成長主要受惠於處分銅鑼廠房予美 光認列一次性利益及本業
營運由虧轉盈。  
2. 力積電 1Q26 合併營收表現強勁，受惠於 1 月起邏輯代工調漲 15-30% 及 6 
月記憶體結構性漲價效應，毛利率逐季走揚。在  Intel EMIB 先進封裝大單
與 AI 電源管理晶片雙引擎帶動下，12 吋產能全線滿載，淘汰低毛利產品，
獲利品質發生本質性躍升。  
3. 2026 年確立為力積電轉型為「 3D AI Foundry 」的元年。公司積極推行資
產輕量化，處分 P5 廠予美光以大幅優化現金流，並承接美光 HBM 後製程
（PWF）代工，從競爭轉為共生；另與印度塔塔集團 簽署技術移轉，授權  
28 奈米製程，擺脫傳統週期性晶圓廠標籤。  
4. 產品組合中以邏輯代工為獲利支柱，2026 年營收占比預估提升至 55%。該
業務除受惠車用與 AI 影像回溫外，AI 伺服器對高功率 PMIC 的剛性需求使 
12 吋鋁製程平台供不應求；公司透過 Open Foundry 通用平台提高 IDM 客
戶轉單成本，築起強固的防禦屏障。  
5. 公司積極切入美系 AI 供應鏈，針對 EMIB 封裝開發的 IPD 整合被動元件與 
3D 堆疊技術已獲驗證，並於  2026 年 4 月正式放量。預計年底月產能達  
3,000 片，2027 年將擴至 8,000 片；此外在 CPO 與矽光子領域已針對光源
開發兩三年，正由 8 吋轉向 12 吋製程研究。  
6. 力 積 電 首 創 將 高 密 度 矽 電 容 技 術 導 入 通 用 平 台 ， 其 電 容 值 密 度 高 達 >
1000 nF/mm2，能有效解決 AI 加速器高頻雜訊並穩定電源供應。該產品具
備 5-8 層厚銅金屬層以確保優異訊號完整性，現已獲國際大廠驗證並成功
打入英特爾 EMIB 先進封裝生態系。  
7. 力積電運用獨家「晶圓堆疊（WoW）」與晶圓鍵合技術，支援 4-8 層的特
殊邏輯與利基型記憶體晶圓深度異質整合。該技 術能提供 1-8TB/s 的極限
高頻寬與超大連接通道，最關鍵的是資料移動能耗大幅降至  < 1 pJ/bit，是 
Edge AI 進入記憶體的重要關鍵。

### Page 4

力積電三大產品線點火，驅動營
運獲利成長

未來展望  
 1. 力積電主要成長動能全面聚焦於三大方向：首先是記憶體代工特殊加值化
與長約結構性漲價；其次為邏輯代工產能調度優化、實施 15-30% 漲價
與產品升級；最後為 3D AI 晶圓堆疊（WoW）技術、高密度矽電容與高
階先進封裝。  
2. 公司靠著在 ≤ 1Gb 利基型 DRAM 獲全球 71% 的市占率，掌握定價權。
受惠於 Edge AI 長約剛性需求，針對長約客戶進行結構性調整，使雙位
數漲價效益於 2026 年 6 月起全面顯現，帶動毛利率逐季走揚。  
3. 受惠 AI 伺服器對高功率電源管理 IC（PMIC）強勁需求，新客戶導入與
投片量超乎預期，與利基記憶體共築 12 吋產能滿載。公司積極汰除低毛
利產品，將產線由處分予美光的 P5 廠移回新竹廠區進行線縮與製程優
化，帶動價格上漲。  
4. 3D AI 晶圓堆疊（WoW）與先進封裝為第三大成長動能，聚焦先進矽中
介層、整合被動元件（IPD）及 WoW 等 3D AI 產品線。其中 IPD 技術已
獲驗證，並於 2026 年 4 月擴大放量打入 Intel 的 EMIB 生態系，預計 
2026 年 3D AI 營收占比將由 3.2% 成長至 5%。  
5. 在先進記憶體方面，20 奈米高階特殊記憶體預計 2026 年底完成技術開
發，1H27 正式提供產能，初期月產能規劃為 1,000 片。同時，目前正與
國際大廠深度進行特殊記憶體製程移轉研發，預計於竹科廠區優先建立特
殊試產線，逐步建立初始約 5,000 片的產能。  
6. 將 P5 廠處分予美光後，雙方由競爭轉為共生，力積電承接美光之 HBM 
後製程（PWF）代工服務。 2027 年正式開始試產，目標在 4Q27 起達成
量產，長線月產能目標為 2 萬片，此業務毛利穩定且完全不受 DRAM 現
貨價格波動影響。  
7. IPD 矽電容具備 > 1000 nF/mm2 全球領先電容密度，於 2026 年 4 月正
式放量，年底月產能達 3,000 片，2027 年將擴至 8,000 片。先進矽中介
層雖因 P5 廠移轉短暫停線，但隨即於 2Q25 至 3Q26 在新竹廠區復線，
目前 12 吋產能已全線滿載迎接 2H26 旺季。  
8. 記憶體代工產能透過製程優化，維持在每月約 4 萬至 4.1 萬片。而邏輯代
工因部分產線移回新竹，使總體傳統產能趨於輕量化限縮。力積電 2026 
年多個季度邏輯產品的投片需求已明確大於實際可供產能，供不應求市況
明顯。

個股投資建議與目標價三年歷史趨勢：
